2. RECOGNITION 
A. Troy High School – Lemelson-MIT InvenTeam TM Grant Award 
Troy High School physics te acher, John Morrison, and physics club students won 
InvenTeam in this year's prestigious Le melson-MIT STEM competition for their 
innovative mechanical-assist stair climbe r project. The Troy High School team; 
Sandeep Arakali, David Bai, Nitin Chandr a, Allen Chen, Kathy Chen, Matthew Cui, 
Rolland He, Lawrence Kim, Maxwell Li, Richard Mu, Nikhil Ramu, Phillip Yang, Clark 
Zhang, and Arnold Zhou are just one of 15  high school teams across the country to 
be awarded $10,000 to develop a prototype of their project . In June, the students 
will showcase their stair climber at Eureka Fest on the MIT campus in Cambridge, 
Massachusetts. 
Would the students please come forward at this time?

 President Philippart called the meeting to order at 
7:00 p.m. 
In addition to Ms. Philippart, present were Board 
members Edmunds, Fleming,  Hauff, Malven Miletti, 
and Underwood. Two high school student

additional members of the audience.   
RECOGNITION 
Heather Kelly, Visual Arts 
Teacher: Art Prize 2011 
This year, artwork by Troy High art teacher Heather 
Kelly, was chosen for display at Art Prize in Grand 
Rapids, which is one of the country’s most prestigious 
annual art exhibitions.  A brief video was shown of 
Ms. Kelly’s interview regarding her piece of work.  
Her artwork was a mixed media piece entitled “Wish 
Project”.  Photos of her artwork were also shared 
with the audience.   
Rebecca Brewer, “2011 ING 
Award 
Rebecca Brewer, an advance placement biology 
teacher at Troy High School , was recognized as the 
nation’s most innovative  educator in the 2011 ING 
Unsung Heroes® Awards Program.  Through this 
program, ING awards ed ucators throughout the 
United States grants for innovative teaching ideas. 
Rebecca was presented wi th a check for $27,000 to 
help fund her award-winning project, “Mini-Med 
School – Simulating Surgeries” at Troy High School.  
Her project was designed to move 11th and 12th 
grade biology students from dissecting specimens to

CURRICULUM 
iPad Curriculum/Lesson 
Technology and Merri Lynn Colligan, Director of 
Technology presented an overview of the integration 
of iPads in the classroom , the iPad integration 
lessons and support resources developed by the iPad 
Integration Committee.  Ms. Colligan stated that the 
iPads were purchased through 2004 technology bond 
monies as part of the laptop refresh to address the 
Learners to keep up with international technology 
standards. The rollout of  the iPads began at the 
elementary level and will be introduced to the 
secondary level next.  This was accomplished 
through the collaborative effort of teachers who 
formed a committee to inte grate the core curriculum 
into the use of the iPad devices and have 
downloaded many apps that are resourceful in 
teaching some of their lessons.  Mr. Keoleian stated 
that even kindergartners are successfully using these 
devices. Ms. Colligan stated that afterschool 
professional development sessions will be held to 
train teachers, media specialists, para-pros and 
administrators on the devices as well as using a blog 
and Moodle to share obstacles or advancements

Assessment, shared a PowerP oint presentation with 
the Board regarding some of the upcoming changes 
that will be made in our state assessment system.  
She stated that we will be seeing an increase in the 
cut scores for proficienc y on the MEAP and MME 
tests.  She said that the in crease in the cut score is 
meant to more accurately reflect whether or not a 
Additionally, she stated, th at in 2015, we will first 
administer the Smarter Balance computer adaptive 
assessments measuring the Common Core State 
Standards and the benefits of  that type of testing.  
Ms. Mull also reviewed the timeline for transitioning 
from current standards of assessment to the new 
methods, which will be fu lly implemented by 2015.  
She stated that in 2012 the MEAP would be minimally 
modified and that the last MEAP will be given the fall 
of 2013.  She indicated th at according to the old 
standards students only n eeded to get 39% of the 
answers correct but with the new standards they 
would need to get 65% correct.  She added that 
even though student test sc ores will initially fall that 
they will eventually rise ag ain.  She said that this 
shift will better reflect whether or not schools are 
preparing their students to be on track for being 
career and/or college ready after they complete high 
school.  Ms. Mull also me ntioned that student tests 
would be scored by artifici al intelligence and that we 
would see faster results and shorter test lengths 
along with increased precisi on, which will be tailored 
to student ability.

everyone posted

confirmed that this is all the information we have and 
that the State Board of Ed ucation’s decision is to 
continue testing social studies and science using the

OTHER 
Facilities Study Update 
Facilities Study Team co-chairs, Greg Bryen and 
Waqar Hashim gave a brief overview of the District’s 
facilities study process and announced the members 
of the recently-appointed Facilities Study Team, 
which is comprised of 18 community members.  Mr. 
Bryen indicated that the members bring a collective 
knowledge to the team with a wide range of 
experiences and backgrounds from engineers to 
financial experts so they are off to a good start.   
They shared a PowerPoint  presentation with the 
Board showing the team’s roles and responsibilities 
and timeline of the process.   T h e y  s t a t e d  t h a t  t h e  
team met on October 28 and 29 for general training 
and team-building skills.  They developed ground 
rules in order to remain fo cused and on-task.  They 
stated that they would be analyzing District 
resources, operating costs,  enrollment projections, 
and trends as well as capacity planning, building use, 
transportation issues, speci al needs, and many other 
areas over the next several months and plan to 
present their findings to the Board at the February 7, 
2012 Board of Education Meeting.  He stated that the 
team is dedicated to this challenge and are prepared 
Troy Board of Education Meeting 7 November 1, 2011 
12
to devote their personal time to help with this very 
important initiative.   
for the huge commitment they have made in 
volunteering to help with this process. 
Ms. Philippart thanked them and stated that she is 
confident in how the study is being managed by Stu 
Redpath and Mike Zinn and that Mr. Zinn has 
represented other districts through this same process 
successfully.

6. A. PERSONNEL 
 CONSIDERATION:  EXECUTION OF MESSA AGREEMENT 
WHEREAS, Section 22f(1) of the State School  Aid Act, 2011 PA 62, provides for 
incentive payments to school districts that meet at least four of the five “best 
practices”, including being the policyholder for each of its insurance policies providing 
for medical, pharmacy, dental, vision, disability, long-term care, or any other type of 
benefit that would constitute a healthcare services benefit to its employees and their 
dependents; and 
WHEREAS, the attached “Employer Particip ation Agreement for MESSA Group 
Welfare Benefit Programs,” hereby incorp orated within this Resolution, expressly 
acknowledges and provides that the Troy School District be the policyholder of such 
MESSA group welfare benefit programs; and 
WHEREAS, this Board of Education deems execut ion of said “Employer Participation 
Agreement for MESSA Group Welfare Benefit Programs” to be in the best interests of 
the Troy School District,  
It is recommended that the Board of Educat ion accepts and agrees to the terms, and 
hereby authorizes its Superintendent or her designee, to execute said “Employer 
Participation Agreement for MESSA Group Welf are Benefit Programs” on behalf of the 
Troy School District 
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Acceptance of the resolution below: 
SUGGESTED RESOLUTION 
 Moved by         
 Seconded by        
BE IT THEREFORE RESOLVED that the Board of Education accepts and agrees to the terms, 
and hereby authorizes its Superintendent or her designee, to execute said “Employer 
Participation Agreement for MESSA Group Welfare Benefit Programs” on behalf of the Troy 
School District

6. B. PERSONNEL 
 CONSIDERATION:  POLICYHOLDER RESOLUTION 
WHEREAS, Section 22f(1) of the State School Aid Act, 2011 PA 62, MCL 388.1622f(1) 
provides for incentive payments from the state school aid fund to those school districts 
that, prior to June 1, 2012, meet at least four of the five “bes t practices”, including 
being the policyholder for each of its in surance policies providing for medical, 
pharmacy, dental, vision, disability, long-ter m care, or any other type of benefit that 
would constitute a healthcare services benefit to its employees and their dependents; 
and 
WHEREAS, the Troy School District presently provides such insurance coverage for its 
eligible employees by purchasing same through the Michigan Education Special 
S e r v i c e s  A s s o c i a t i o n ,  “ M E S S A ”  a n d ,  a s  o f  J a n u a r y  1 ,  2 0 1 2 ,  B l u e  C r o s s  B l u e  S h i e l d  
(BCBS), ADN, National Vision Administrators (NVA), and Reliance Standard; and 
WHEREAS, the District is already and will continue  to be policyholders of its health 
insurance plans not administered by MESSA, and MESSA has declared and advised that 
the public school districts purchasing su ch insurance coverage for their employees 
from MESSA are the policyholders of said  insurance plans, within the meaning of 
Section 22f(1) of the State School Aid Act, 2011 PA 62, MCL 388.1622f(1); and 
WHEREAS, this Board of Education deems it in the best interests of the Troy School 
District to be the policyholder of each of  its insurance policies providing healthcare 
services benefits to its employees and their dependents,  
It is recommended that the Troy School Di strict shall be the policyholder, within the 
meaning of Section 22f(1) of  the State School Aid Act, 2011 PA 62, MCL 388.1622f(1), 
as is, and as may be amended, for any and all insurance policies providing healthcare 
services benefits to its employees and th eir dependents, including all MESSA branded 
plans. 
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Acceptance of the resolution below: 
SUGGESTED RESOLUTION 
 Moved by          
 Seconded by          
BE IT THEREFORE RESOLVED  that the Troy School District shall be the policyholder, within 
the meaning of Section 22f(1) of the State School Aid Act, 2011 PA 62, MCL 388.1622f(1), as is, 
and as may be amended, for any and all insurance policies providing healthcare services 
benefits to its employees and their dependents, including all MESSA branded plans.

A. Full-Day Kindergarten 
See Memo… 
Recommended Action:  This is a report. 
 Dr. Fowler will present the background  on our considerat ion of Full-Day 
Kindergarten and will introduce Dr. Joan Lessen-Firestone. 
 Dr. Joan Lessen-Firestone will pres ent a summary of the research and 
rationale behind Full-Day Kindergarten, as well as the benefits of Full-Day 
programming. 
 Mrs. Jan Keeling will present a proposed draft timeline for the implementation 
of Full-Day Kindergarten. 
93
Memorandum 
 DATE:  December 1, 2011 
 TO:  Dr. Barbara Fowler 
Assistant Superintendent 
  Assistant Superintendent, Elementary Instruction 
4400 Livernois 
248/823-4000,  
Fax - 248/823-4013 
The pending legislation that would change the State’s fund ing of kindergarten students 
caused us to seriously consider offering full-day kindergarten for the 2012-2013 school 
year.   
In October, I made a report to the Board of Education that included:  
 the results of surveying Oakland County districts on their intended kindergarten 
programming for 2012-2013; 
 a summary of research that supports the benefits of full-day programming; 
 a cost comparison between the cost of implementing full-day kindergarten and of 
not implementing full-day kindergarten and potentially losing half of the 
foundation allowance behind each kindergartner; 
 the impact of Troy’s changing demographics on kindergarten programming; 
 and the impact of the new Common Core State Standards on kindergarten 
programming. 
The Board of Education asked me to prepare a proposed timeline for implementation of 
full-day kindergarten, showing the other decisions impacted by this decision, which I 
presented at the November Board Workshop. 
While the initial trigger for exploring full-day kindergarten was financial, it clearly is what 
is best for our young learners.  Tonight we have invited Dr. Lessen-Firestone, Director of  
Early Childhood at Oakland Schools and nationally recognized leader in the field, to 
present “Full- Day Kindergarten: Why Does it Matter?”

                7. B.     12.06.11 
7. CURRICULUM 
B.   REPORT:  Summer Success Academy  
This past summer the Troy School District provided an extended 
learning opportunity for 49 students that would be entering 9th grade 
for the 2011-2012 school year.  The purpose of the Summer Success 
Academy was to provide support for students making the transition 
from Baker, Boulan Park, Larson and Smith Middle Schools to Athens 
and Troy High Schools.  This is the first time the Troy School District 
offered a program of this kind, which offered students an opportunity 
to strengthen math, English and study skills prior to the vital 
transition to high school.  The goal of the Summer Success Academy 
was to reduce the number of studen ts failing one or more courses 
during their 9 th grade year and to jump-s tart their success in high 
school through intensive grade level instruction.  
_________________________________________________________________ 
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  This is a report. 
Dr. Richard Machesky, Assistant Superintendent for Secondary Instruction 
will provide the report this evening.

8. BUSINESS  
A. CONSIDERATION:  BEST PRACTICES INCENTIVE 
  Attached is a resolution for Board co nsideration regarding the Best Practices 
Incentive with the Michigan Department of Education. 
______________________________________________________   
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Adopt the resolution below. 
 Moved by:       __________________________ 
 Supported by:  __________________________ 
WHEREAS, Section 22f of the State School Aid Act provides $100 per pupil one-
time grants to districts that satisfy at least 4 of 5 best practices criteria not later than 
June 1, 2012 (MCL 388.1622f) and; 
WHEREAS, the Board of Education of Troy School District desires to receive the 
$100 per pupil incentive payment and; 
WHEREAS, the Troy School District has satisf ied at least 4 of 5 best practices 
criteria and; 
WHEREAS, eligibility for the incentive payment is contingent upon adopting a 
resolution that states the District has complie d with at least 4 of 5 of the best practices 
criteria;  
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED AS FOLLOWS: 
1. The Board of Education of Troy School District certifies that the District 
has complied with the following requirements: 
 The District is the designated policyholder for medical benefit plan(s) 
pursuant to Section 22f(1)(b). 
 The District will continue to implem ent a Service Consolidation Plan 
pursuant to Section 22f(1)(c).  A copy of the School Board signed 
resolution agreeing to develop a Se rvice Consolidation Plan was sent 
to the MDE on September 13, 2011.  We agree to send MDE a status 
report on the development/implementation of the plan by February 1 
each year. 
123
 The District has obtained competitive bids on non-instructional 
services pursuant to Section 22f(1)(d). 
 The District will provide a link on the District’s home page for the URL 
for the MI School Data Portal , which will contain the required 
dashboard indicators pursuant to Section 22f(1)(e).  If certain data 
elements for our District are unavailable from State data collections, 
we agree to provide those data in the form and manner determined by 
the MDE. 
2. The Board of Education of Troy School District authorizes and directs its 
secretary to file this resolution with the State Aid and School Finance 
Office of the Michigan Department of Education. 
3. All resolutions and parts of resoluti ons insofar as they conflict with the 
provisions of this resolution are hereby rescinded.

A. REPORT:   Facilities Study Update 
The following is an overview of the Di strict’s facilities study process being 
undertaken by the Facilities Study Team comprised of 18 community 
members.  This study will result in r ecommendations that will be presented 
to the Board at the February 7, 2012 Board of Education Meeting.  The 
District would like to thank the indi viduals on the study team for their 
willingness to participate in this important undertaking.

10.  OTHER 
B. CONSIDERATION:  NSBA Annual Federal Relations Network  
 Conference Attendance 
As required by Board Policy B- 2001, Workshops an d Conferences, 
“…expenditure of district  funds for members for out-of-state meetings 
must have prior approval by resoluti on.”  Board President, Ms. Philippart, 
has indicated her desire to attend the National School Boards Association’s 
Annual Federal Relations Network (FRN) Conference, which will be held in 
Washington, D.C., from February 5 - 7, 2012. Troy Schools was one of six 
districts selected by Oakland County School Board Association to receive a 
$500 scholarship as reimbursement if  a Board member attends this 
conference.  We, therefore, ask you to consider the following resolution.   
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Adopt the following resolution. 
SUGGESTED RESOLUTION 
   M o v e d  b y   
   S u p p o r t e d  b y   
Be it therefore resolved  that the Board of Education takes action to approve 
Ms. Philippart’s attendance as representative of the Troy Board of Education at the 
National School Boards Association’s Annual Federal Relations Network Conference 
in Washington, D.C., from February 5 - 7, 2012.
